| 448 | Owned copies, and a lower rate to suggest (2026-10-22; item 1 of 5: the database) | The owner: "a counter on each card that tells the worker how many of that card we likely own (CC database + all copies paid/ours)": two numbers on the Price screen, between the card's details and its price, this printing and this card across every printing; pointing at it, a popup in the Price Warning's style, "We own X copies of this printing and Y copies of this card", suggesting a lower rate the worker can click to apply, never applied by itself. Answers (2026-10-22): the printing counts the same finish only; Magic only for now (Pokémon has no CC matching yet); 5% off per copy of the card over 2, as a percent of the card's rate (Credit 60% / Cash 40% with 4 owned: 54% / 36%), from the rate it would get now, up to 50% off (12 copies); applied, it goes on ADD CARD as the card's own rate (Set Rate). Then the owner: "add a checkbox for "Auto apply rates?" under this new element. When checked it remains checked for the whole buy or project until confirmed. And it automatically applies the custom rate math to any/all applicable cards added to the buy list." Answers: kept with the buy (a reload or another computer keeps it); walk-in buys until CONFIRM BUY and overnight buys while they can be edited, not projects (they have no rates); the phone's scans get it too; checking it leaves the cards already on the list as they are. Shipped as five items: (1) the database, (2) the counter, (3) the popup and its suggestion, (4) applying it, (5) Auto apply rates?. **Item 1, migration 0008:** the inventory upload keeps each product's **Total Qty** (`ccNames.js` `totalQty`, `cc_products.total_qty`; a file uploaded before has none, so CC copies count once the inventory is uploaded again). `owned_copies` counts one printing's and one card's copies: the current inventory's Total Qty (the printing's product as the export matches it; the card's by its names as the export searches them, not Art Series, a count below zero as none) plus the Magic cards that are ours and not in the inventory yet (not bulk, and not placeholders, though the header's search lists them: nothing ever exports or completes a placeholder, so it would count for good and lower the rate): a confirmed walk-in's or a Paid collection's card, not struck, not stamped by an export, not marked done; and any card exported to CC after the inventory was uploaded (`cc_exported_at`), a Completed collection's too. A card copied to Can't upload cards or Bulk pulled from exports counts once, as its copy. No inventory, or one uploaded before 0008, reads as unknown, not 0. The app can't tell when CC's CSV was taken, so cards exported between taking it and uploading it count as in it. SAVE EDIT folds a card on its own rate only into a line at that same rate, so an edit can't spread its rate over another line's copies or lose it; a card with none still folds into one with its own rate and takes it (0056, owner 2026-10-15), a line with none first; and a copy added with no own rate joins a line with none before one at its own rate. The database test run now runs in two passes (the restore checks on their own), as one pass outgrew the 100 s the API waits. `draft_add_line` and `collection_add_line` take a card's own rate in the same write (checked as Set Rate checks it; a project refuses one); a card with its own rate joins only a line at that rate, one without joins as before. Each buy keeps its Auto apply rates? box (`buys.auto_rates`): a walk-in's set by `draft_set_auto_rates`, an overnight buy's as one of its details through `collection_update_info` (in the Changelog: "auto apply rates: off → on"); a project refuses it. |

Make sure to include it in the bottom hotkey hint text." Alt+A wasn't used anywhere in the app. With the cursor in the Price screen's search bar (where the other keys work), **Alt+A** switches mimp mode on or off, as clicking 🦇 or 🌼 does (decision 323). The hint line under the price panel ends "· Alt+A mimp mode", and the pill's tooltips say "(Alt+A)". On a window too narrow for the whole hint line it's cut short with "…" as before, and this, being last, goes first (`onKeyDown`, `PricingScreen.jsx`; `SearchBar.jsx`). |
| 446 | A name typed without accents finds the cards that have them (2026-10-22) | The owner: "Cards like Nazgûl have special characters in their names, letters with accent characters and do not come up in seaches for "nazgul". Can we make sure our search matches nazgul to Nazgûl automatically without making loading times for searches longer please." Neither Scryfall nor TCGdex folds accents: "nazgul" finds nothing on Scryfall, "nazgûl" its 3 printings; TCGdex has no "flabebe" (checked 2026-10-22). The card-name lists the typo correction already loads (each kept 7 days, loaded as the app starts) now also give, once loaded, every accented word under its plain spelling. **Magic:** a word typed without accents, of 3 letters or more, is searched beside the accented words whose plain spelling contains it, up to 4 ("nazgul" → `("nazgul" or "Nazgûl")`, "lim dul" → `"lim" ("dul" or "Dûl" or …)`). **Pokémon (English):** TCGdex takes one name, so each plain word that is exactly one accented word is sent as that word ("flabebe" → "Flabébé", "pokemon center lady" → "Pokémon center lady"). No request is added or waited for: before the list has loaded (the very first start, or a new day's download), a search goes as typed, as before. A word typed with its accents goes as typed. Japanese Pokémon names aren't affected (`accentedWords`, `spellingsOf`, `withAccents`, `normalize.js`; `nameTerms`, `printsQuery`, `scryfall.js`; `searchCards`, `tcgdex.js`). |

| 443 | Magic printouts sorted set > colour > name > number (2026-10-22) | The owner: "The current main format is: Set > Collectors number, which I thought would be a good match because our inventory is set > color > alphabetical. BUT it's not close enough. Now that we have a set > color > alphabetical sort for the buy list now though, we might as well reuse that for exports too … (so that different printings of the same cards show up together. For tied cards, then put collectors number as the last sort so: set > color > alphabetical > collectors number)." Every Magic audit printout (Print MTG (all) and (>…), Print collection, Print buy, the print after CONFIRM BUY) keeps its sections, one per Crystal Commerce category A to Z with Unmatched last. Inside a section the rows go by the buy list's WUBRG piles (decision 403): white, blue, black, red, green, multicolor, colourless, lands, then a card with no Scryfall card. In each pile they go by name A to Z, then collector number, then the same printing's conditions (NM, LP, MP, HP, DMG), then non-foil, foil, etched. They used to go NM by collector number, then the other conditions by collector number. The pile is the row's own colour chip (`cardBucket`), so the chips run in blocks; it differs from the buy list's only for a double-faced card with a land on its back, which the chip calls a land. Print PKM? and the stickers sort as before (`byPile`, `auditPrint.js`). |
| 442 | A click on a menu group keeps its flyout open (2026-10-22) | The owner: "The "hierarchical menu" menus we have for print etc close when clicked, just have them ignore clicks and stay open, they already open on hover." A group in a ⋯ or EXPORT ▾ menu (◂ Print, ◂ Export, ◂ Convert; decisions 422, 425) opened its flyout on hover and toggled it on a click, so the click that followed the hover closed it. A click now only opens it (from the keyboard, Enter still does) and never closes it; hovering another item, the arrow key back, or the menu closing still do (`GroupRow`, `MenuItems.jsx`). |
| 441 | Undo last pick in the Export dialog (2026-10-22) | The second part of decision 440's ask. Once a product or None of these has been picked in **Needs a choice**, the dialog's footer shows **Undo last pick** at its left, with the card and what it was given ("1 Lightning Bolt (M10) 146 [NM] → Lightning Bolt"). It sits in the footer so that its appearing never moves the cards. Pressed, the pick is taken back: the card, and every card of the same printing and finish the pick covered, go back to how they were, the card in Needs a choice with nothing ticked (even while it's held). Pressed again, it takes back the pick before that, back to the first one since the dialog opened or last matched. On EXPORT and Export MTG csv nothing is saved before the export, so Undo only changes the dialog. In the check run, which remembers each pick at once, Undo also puts back what was remembered for that printing before (a stale remembered product put back as it was, stamped now), or forgets it; the pick's and Undo's writes go one after another. A ticked "Always use the picked product's category for <set>" stays saved (nothing removes one), and Undo says so ("Undone. <set> keeps the category it was given."). Picks made with Change in Matched aren't in it: Change has its own Cancel (`remember`, `undo`, `inOrder`, `dbLinks`, `ExportDialog.jsx`; `.xd-undo`, `export.css`). |
| 440 | A pick in Needs a choice holds, then folds away (2026-10-22) | The owner: "When confirming a CC card match version on the Export screen, when clicked, it goes away so fast and the new item appears in the spot your mouse is so quickly it's easy for human error to misclick selecting a version without looking … can we manually slow down the way the list moves up somehow AND add a quick undo button". Two parts, this one first (the Undo is decision 441). A product or None of these picked in the Export dialog's **Needs a choice** (EXPORT, Export MTG csv and the check run alike) still applies at once, but the card, and any other card of the same printing and finish the pick covered, stays where it was for **half a second** (the owner, after the first build's 1 second: "Make this 0.5 seconds"): greyed, its pick checked, a green "✓ Picked: <product>" (or "✓ None of these") at its top right, and not clickable (`inert`). Then it folds away over 0.3 s, so the next card slides up rather than jumping under the pointer. Meanwhile Needs a choice's count leaves it out and Continue stays greyed. With reduced motion set in Windows, it vanishes after the half second without the fold. A card changed with Change in Matched stays in Matched, as before (`HOLD_MS`, `FOLD_MS`, `hold`, `ExportDialog.jsx`; `.xd-fold`, `.xd-choose.held`, `.xd-held-note`, `export.css`). |
| 439 | Who may use the backup key (2026-10-22) | The last part of decision 436. `todaysPrices` sends `backup: true` only when its caller says so, and five do: **EXPORT**'s Sell Prices (`ccExport.js` `sellPrices`), a Pokémon day's **COMPLETE** (`DayPage.jsx` `complete`), every audit printout, **Print MTG / Print PKM / Print collection / Print buy** and the one offered after CONFIRM BUY (`useAuditPrint.js`), the **sticker sheets** (`useStickerPrint.js`) and a collection's **REPRICE?** (`RepriceModal.jsx`; the owner: "allow reprice as well"). The Price screen (its prices and searches, `prices.js`, and the phone scanner's) never sends it, so when the main key is out it shows the limit as before. |
| 438 | `prices` moves to the backup key when the main one is out (2026-10-22) | The third part of decision 436. A `prices` call sent with `backup: true` runs on the main key as before until JustTCG answers `DAILY_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` or `REQUEST_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` (the monthly cap counts too, the owner: "yes"); then, if a backup key is saved, that request and the rest of the call go to the backup key, and what the main key already found is kept. Without the flag, or with no backup key, or once the backup is out as well, the call answers 429 as before. Each call tries the main key first, so the backup stops being used as soon as the main key works again (00:00 UTC for the daily cap). On the backup, `POST /cards` goes 20 cards at a time (the Free plan's limit; a 100-card batch the main key was refused is sent again as five), and its usage isn't written to the Settings meter. The Free plan's 100 requests a day go further when the 6-hour cache already has the cards. Not done: the app isn't told the backup was used (the function's log says so). `ask`, `OnBackup`, `BACKUP_BATCH` (`supabase/functions/prices/index.ts`); the function is redeployed. |

| 430 | The pre-push review's two data-loss paths (2026-10-21) | The pre-push review of decisions 426–429 confirmed two, and the owner: "keep CONFIRM BUY open while a lost answer waits, undo the paged backup (back to the one-call backup_export, F2 reopened)". **CONFIRM BUY** (decision 427's N3): the dialog closed whenever the draft emptied, including after this computer's own press lost its answer, when the draft reloads empty and the next press must read that buy back (decisions 367, 374); with the dialog gone, BUY COMPLETE, the 🚩 and the next buy's confirm went wrong. Download backup is the one call to `backup_export` again; its rare timeout fails out loud and is pressed again. No migration: the setting is a `settings` row (`bulk_export_rates`), and the Changelog shows its name from the app (`targetOf`, `changelog.js`), since migration 0006 names only the settings it knew (`src/lib/bulkCut.js`; `useBulkCut`, `settings.jsx`; `BulkExportRatesPanel.jsx`; `auditPdf.js`, `useAuditPrint.js`, `ExportDialog.jsx`, `ExportButton.jsx`, `BuyPanel.jsx`, `DayPage.jsx`, `CollectionPage.jsx`). |
| 415 | The phone's Undo after a network error stays "undoing" (2026-10-20) | The last open part of decision 392, on the owner's ask. An Undo whose removal failed at once with a network error (not the 10 s limit) was answered "couldn't take it off", though it may have gone through, and FOIL? / NONFOIL? then sent no new copy. Now a removal says when its answer was lost (true, false for a refusal, null for no answer), and the phone's Undo treats a lost answer as it treats the time limit: left "undoing" and claimed again, when its request id takes the copy off once or says it's done. A refusal (no user, the card gone) is still said to the phone (`remove`, `useDraft.js` and `useCollection.js`; `undo`, `useScanReceiver.js`). |
| 414 | A new inventory over the old one clears the old one in batches (2026-10-20) | The owner hit "Saving the upload failed: canceling statement due to statement timeout" uploading a new Master Crystal Inventory over the old one, and asked for the batched fix. Saving the upload deleted the old file and, in the same statement, its ~152,000 products: 18.9 s on the dev copy with both files loaded, over the 8 s the app's statements get. Now the new file becomes current first (instant), then the old file's products go 5,000 at a time ("Clearing the old file…"), each call well under the limit (a first batch of 20,000 took 4.8 s on dev, later ones about 1 s), and the old file's row goes once it's empty. Exports never match against a half-cleared inventory. If the clearing is cut short, the next upload finishes it. A failed upload is undone the same way. Two uploads at once: once the newer one is current, the one started earlier is refused ("upload_gone") and cleared with the rest, as before. Migration 0007: `master_inventory_finish` drops only older files with no products left, and it and `cc_products_load` refuse an upload started before the current file; `master_inventory_prune` (`pruneProducts`, `uploadCsv`, `src/lib/masterInventory.js`; `MasterInventoryPanel.jsx`). |

| 395 | CANCEL finds the buy a scan opened (2026-10-20) | Left from decision 350: a walk-in scan with no buy open opens one for itself, which the computer doesn't load until its next write; when that scan never adds (it needs a price, or fails), CANCEL on the computer saw no buy, did nothing, and the scan stayed waiting for a buy nobody would confirm. Now CANCEL with no buy loaded looks up this computer's draft and, if it has no cards, cancels it, failing its scans with "The buy was cancelled: scan it again for the next buy" as any cancel does; one with cards (another tab's, which drafts share, spec 8.12) is loaded instead and CANCEL asks as usual, never thrown away unseen (`cancel`, `useDraft.js`). No database change. |
| 394 | Settings saves: a new key's first, and two boxes of one setting (2026-10-20) | Left from decision 354. **Two first saves of a new key at once** both found no row and both passed, the second overwriting the first; now the second waits for the first and is refused like any out-of-date save (`settings_save`, migration 0004). **A quick Tab between two boxes of one object setting** (Fallback, Tiers, Bulk) was refused as out of date: the second box saved against the version from before the first box's save. It was also built on the setting as the screen last showed it, so had it gone through it would have written the first box's old value back. Now one setting's saves go one at a time, each built on the value saved just before it (this screen's own save, until the reload brings it), and the panels hand `save` a change to make rather than a whole value (`save`, `settings.jsx`; `FallbackPanel.jsx`, `RatesTiersPanel.jsx`, `BulkValuesPanel.jsx`). |
| 393 | Convert leaves a Completed game whole (2026-10-20) | Left from decision 353: a game whose cards are Completed is locked whole on a walk-in buy, but Convert to project and Convert to collection moved its bulk and placeholders (which are never Completed themselves) off it. Now a game with any Completed card stays on the buy whole, its bulk, placeholders and struck cards too; only the other game's lines move (`buy_to_project`, `buy_to_collection`, migration 0004; `convertLines`, `dayRules.js`, for the ⋯'s greyed reason, "every card is Completed, struck, or in a Completed game", and the dialog, which says that game's bulk and placeholders stay). |
| 392 | The phone's Undo and FOIL? / NONFOIL?, once each and said (2026-10-20) | The rest of the full review's B3. **FOIL? / NONFOIL? could leave no copy:** the phone gave up after 30 s and forgot the swap, but the computer could still take the old copy off after that (its own limit is also 30 s), and then the new one was never sent. Now at 30 s the phone frees its buttons and says the new copy goes in when the old one is off, and still sends it when that happens (`refinish`, `Scanner.jsx`). **An Undo given up still took a copy off:** the computer, past its 30 s, told the phone the card was still there, but the removal could still run; now a given-up Undo takes nothing off (`undo`'s `live`, `useScanReceiver.js`). **A refused plain Undo said nothing:** the scan went back to "added" with the computer's note (no user, the card gone), and the strip showed a tick; now the phone says the note, with the error sound. **An Undo whose answer was lost took two copies off:** its scan, claimed again a minute later, ran the Undo again; now the Undo sends its scan's id as the removal's request id (decision 388), so it takes one copy off once, however often it's claimed. An Undo with no answer in time is no longer told to the phone as "still there" (it may have gone through, which left FOIL? with no copy): it stays "undoing" and is claimed again, and a second FOIL? / NONFOIL? waits until the first's old copy is off (`useScanReceiver.js`, `useDraft.js`, `useCollection.js`; migration 0004). |
| 391 | An edit keeps the scans its line came from (2026-10-20) | Left from decision 351: the phone's scan id on a line (its own, or one merged into it) is how a scan claimed again after a lost answer finds the line it already made (`lineOfScan`). An edit sent a fresh price snapshot with no scan in it, and two lines an edit folded together kept only the surviving line's, so such a scan was added twice. Now `draft_update_line` and `collection_update_line` keep the edited line's scans (in its snapshot's `scans`), and a fold keeps both lines' (`internal.scan_ids`, `internal.with_scans`, migration 0004). |
| 390 | The set choices are read page by page (2026-10-20) | The open item "the rest of B16: Import Collection's remembered picks past 1,000": looked at again, the import's product candidates were already read page by page (decision 346), its remembered picks are read 40 keys at a time (one row a key) and its links a hundred products at a time, so none of those can reach the API's 1,000-row limit. The one read left that could was the set choices staff made ("Always use the picked product's category for…", `cc_set_map`), read whole by the export's matching, Import Collection and Settings → Crystal Commerce matching; a choice past the first 1,000 would have been lost. All three now read it page by page in a fixed order (`readSetMap`, `ccExport.js`; `CcMatchingPanel.jsx`). |
| 389 | Nothing is priced until Settings has loaded (2026-10-20) | The rest of the full review's B15 (decision 364 made a failed first load "not loaded"): meanwhile the Price screen priced with the built-in defaults (rates, Fallback Percentages, tiers, bulk values), which can differ from the store's. The owner chose **block until loaded**. Until Settings loads, ADD CARD and SAVE EDIT (every pricing screen), CONFIRM BUY, the phone's scans (they wait, claimed later), REPRICE?, EXPORT (its Sell Prices), Complete day…, Print PKM Stickers? and Import Collection's save all wait, saying "Settings haven't loaded yet: nothing is priced until they do" (`SETTINGS_WAIT`, `useSettingsReady`, `src/state/settings.jsx`). A first load that failed shows a red bar under the header with **Retry**; it also tries again by itself (decision 364). |
| 388 | Lost answers on flaky wifi: a time limit, and a reload after a short drop (2026-10-20) | The rest of the full review's B18. **No request timeout:** the Supabase client had none, so a request whose answer never came hung as long as the browser let it. Now every request has a limit: 30 s for a database call (the database stops a statement after 8 s, checked on dev: `statement_timeout=8s` for the signed-in role), 120 s for the price function, file uploads and downloads, and the calls that carry a whole backup or file (`restore_backup`, `backup_export`, `cc_products_load`, `collection_import`, `project_import`, `export_lines`); past it the request ends as a lost answer, which every write already handles (`limitFor`, `timedFetch`, `src/lib/timedFetch.js`; `supabase.js`). **A short drop never reloaded:** the connection counted as lost only after 10 s of Realtime down, and only then did screens reload when it came back, so after a shorter drop other computers' changes were missed until something else changed. Now Realtime coming back after any drop reloads what's on screen (`connection.jsx`). **ADD CARD, ×, strike and Create pressed again after a lost answer happened twice:** now each sends a request id, a fresh one for every press except the next press of the same write within two minutes of its lost answer, which sends that one's and then says "Done once: … Press again to do it again" (so a second box of bulk added on purpose still goes in on the press after); the phone's scans have ids of their own and never take or clear staff's; the database does each request id once and gives the first answer again (`internal.done_requests`, kept a day; `p_request` on `draft_add_line`, `draft_remove_line`, `collection_add_line`, `collection_remove_line`, `collection_strike_line`, `buy_strike_line`, `buy_remove_line`, `collection_create` and `project_create`, migration 0004; `requests.js`, `useDraft.js`, `useCollection.js`, `DayPage.jsx`, `CollectionsPage.jsx`). |
| 387 | A Can't upload cards export whose answer was lost is told apart (2026-10-20) | Left from Batch 2 (decision 362): after a lost answer the dialog reads its cards back for this export's id, but a Can't upload cards export deletes the copies it exports, so it could only say the export may have gone through. Those copies' stamps, the export's id in the Sell Price basis included, are written onto the cards they were copied from (`export_lines`), so the dialog now reads those (each copy's `source_line_id`): if they carry its id, the export went through and the file downloads from them; if not, it didn't, and it says so. It still can't tell when a copy's original is gone (its buy or collection deleted) or the connection is still down (`ExportDialog.jsx`). No database change. |
| 386 | A single buy's file again (2026-10-20) | Left from the full review's Batch 2 (decision 361): Export single buy is blocked once the buy is Completed, and the day's EXPORT leaves single-buy cards out, so a single buy's file couldn't be downloaded again. The owner: "Yes, add it." A Magic buy exported on its own now has **Download its file again** in its ⋯: the same file, from its cards' stamps (the "<day>-<number>" Custom SKU), named as Export single buy names it, with no user, lock or change, like the day's and a collection's (`downloadBuyAgain`, `DayPage.jsx`; `BuyPanel.jsx`). Cards a later Can't upload cards export restamped went in that export's file and are left out (decision 372); a buy whose exported cards all went out that way says it has no file of its own. Export single buy's greyed reason and the day's "no day file" message point to it. |

It just gets the next in line, right?" So it stays as built: the buy's old number isn't kept on the project or given again, and the project's new buy is numbered like any CONFIRM BUY. | 384 | Pokémon stickers in whole dollars, 50¢ and $1 at the bottom (2026-10-20) | The owner: "Stickers need to be rounded to the nearest whole dollar. This way we can still use the stickers if we want." (an unsleeved card is 50¢ at the shop, a penny-sleeved one $1). Print PKM Stickers? (decisions 281–283) still reprices as the export does, but a sticker starts from that price before the Sell Price's steps and $0.40 floor (`sellPriceFor`'s new `value`) and prints `stickerPrice` (`src/lib/stickerPdf.js`): up to $0.50 **50¢** (exactly $0.50 too: that's the card's worth), over $0.50 and under $1.00 **$1**, then the nearest whole dollar, half up ($1.49 $1, $1.50 $2, $1,234.56 $1,235); no price at all reads 50¢, as the floor did. The owner's other answers: the line between $1 and the next is $1.00 (so $0.99 prints $1 either way); Complete day's saved Sell Price keeps the old rule (a sticker can read $3 where the green price reads $3.25); the cheap stickers keep the name, number and chip. |
| 383 | The lint hook doesn't block between edits (2026-10-19) | The owner, after another hook error from a change made in two edits: "This is frequently happening because of the new HOOK we added. When those two rules are a file's only errors, `.claude/hooks/lint-edit.mjs` now hands them to Claude as a note (exit 0, `additionalContext`) instead of a blocking error. Any other error, a parse error or `no-use-before-define` among them, still blocks, and the full `npx eslint .` before each commit still fails on any left over. Tried on four sample files in a scratch copy outside the repo: clean passes, the two rules alone give the note, either of them with `no-use-before-define` blocks, a syntax error blocks. The owner approved the edit to the hook. |

| 380 | Prod holds the store's data and nothing else, ever; testing is dev only (2026-10-19) | The owner, after the first migrations after launch: "Make a hard rule for never leaving any trace of test data on prod ever. What follows from it: the database test run refuses to run its checks anywhere but dev (they write DBTEST rows inside a rolled-back transaction and move the counters); on prod it runs only `--structure-only`, which reads the nine fingerprints and nothing else (temp objects inside a rolled-back transaction; no counter touched) for the comparison with a saved dev run; the smoke test, scans and imports are dev's too; the reviewer's brief flags any test or write aimed at prod. The one trace the launch night's full run left on prod (SQUASH.md section 8, before this rule), found by a read-only look the same night: `events_seq_seq` at 162 with no entries, so the first Changelog entry would have been number 163; no DBTEST row anywhere, the buy counter never called. Put back to 1 the same night on the owner's word, by one statement guarded on `events` being empty (it was); prod's first Changelog entry is number 1, dev's counter untouched (9,067). Prod now has no trace of any test. |
| 379 | The buy number counter isn't open to the signed-out role (2026-10-19) | Migration 0003. `buys_number_seq` (0071) was made under Supabase's default privileges for `public`, which grant select, update and usage on a new sequence to anon as well as to the app's roles; `events_seq_seq` never had it. | 378 | The Changelog is written only by the database (2026-10-19) | Decision 274's step 2, SQUASH.md section 11a, the first migration after the squash: 0002. The eight writers (`internal.buy_event`, `internal.collection_event`, `buy_log_cash_download`, `confirm_buy`, `day_complete`, `export_lines`, `export_undo_day`, `project_to_buy`) call it with the values they inserted before, their bodies otherwise unchanged (patched from `supabase/current/`); `restore_backup`, already a definer, keeps its own insert. The signed-in role keeps `select` on `events` and loses insert, update, delete and the rest; the policy "store: append" is dropped; `events_seq_seq` is revoked from it. The app has called a function for every entry since 0064, so nothing in it changes and the v1.0.0-launch build keeps working. The test run checks the definer list, the grants, and that a direct insert and a `nextval` are refused (42501). Reviewed by pug-reviewer (every mapping traced), rehearsed on dev inside a rolled-back transaction with 0003, applied to dev the same night; prod on the owner's word. |

| 374 | A later CONFIRM BUY press keeps the first press's 🚩, and is busy while it reads back (2026-10-19) | The 2026-10-19 push's review, notes 2 and 3, on decision 367. **The 🚩:** when a press after a lost answer found that an earlier press had confirmed the buy, the 🚩 Flag check for condition box was read from the dialog open now, not from the press that went through. Now each press keeps its box with what it sent (`useDraft.js` `confirm`, `flagCondition`), and the flag and BUY COMPLETE follow the press that matched. When both presses match (only the box changed between them), which one went through can't be told, so either one's 🚩 counts: a flag only asks for a check before export. **Busy:** that later press's read-back didn't set busy, so Confirm buy stayed live and another press could show BUY COMPLETE twice (it couldn't confirm twice). Now the button shows Confirming… until the read-back is answered. The owner closed the review's other five notes without a change: two lost answers in a row keep only the second press (1); a list that shrinks between pages can be answered 416, and the page retries (4); TEST.md's case for decision 369 needs a manual or cached price (5); decision 370 also leaves an Import Collection row unmatched (6); "it didn't go through" can be wrong for a very slow save (7). |
| 373 | A paged read with its count takes one request (2026-10-19) | The v0.9.9.15-review release review, note 8, on decision 365. `allPages` reads until a page comes back empty, so a list under 1,000 rows took two requests, and Home, the Collections tab and the Calendar reload on every change to buys. Stopping at a short page instead would break decision 346's promise that a lower Max rows is slower, not wrong (a short page can be the server's limit). Now a read that asks for its row count (`{ count: 'exact' }`) stops once it has them all, under any limit; the rest end as before. The live lists, the Cash buys file and Print from the Collections tab ask for it (`inChunks.js`, `HomePage.jsx`, `CollectionsPage.jsx`, `CalendarPage.jsx`, `cashBuys.js`). SQUASH.md's Max rows note says these reads are paged now. |
| 372 | "Download the same file again" leaves out cards exported from Can't upload cards (2026-10-19) | The v0.9.9.15-review release review, note 7, on decisions 361 and 362. A card that couldn't upload and was later exported from Can't upload cards is restamped on its own buy or collection with that export's Custom SKU and time, so a collection's file again (and a day exported before migration 0071, by its fallback) included it, and re-uploading the file added it twice. The owner, for a collection exported in rounds: **every round of its own** in the file. Now both pages leave out the cards whose stamp time is a Can't upload cards export's: its changelog entry has the same moment as its cards' stamps, both the save's own time, checked on dev (a single-buy export's 20 cards matched to the microsecond). The pages read those times when the file is asked for (`restampTimes`, `ccExport.js`; `withoutRestamps`, `massCreate.js`; `dayExportLines`; `CollectionPage.jsx`, `DayPage.jsx`), so it needs the connection. | 371 | A lost answer is looked at twice before "it didn't go through" (2026-10-19) | The v0.9.9.15-review release review, note 6, on decisions 362 and 363. After an export or a CONFIRM BUY with no answer, the read-back could run before the save, still running on the server, had finished, and the export said "nothing was exported" when it was on its way. Now, when the first look finds nothing, it looks once more two seconds later (the button stays busy meanwhile; `pause`, `SECOND_LOOK_MS` in `src/lib/timeout.js`). Still nothing: the export says "it didn't go through. Try again: the same cards can't be exported twice", which the database guarantees (a second export of the same cards is refused, and the dialog then reads the first one back); CONFIRM BUY shows its error, and pressing again is safe the same way. |
| 370 | The prices function: no old price passed as today's, and an unreadable reply tried once more (2026-10-19) | The v0.9.9.15-review release review, notes 4 and 9, on decision 360. **Old prices:** a remembered card missing from JustTCG's reply was answered with its last kept price, however old, as a normal answer, and the export doesn't check a price's age, so a Sell Price could come from weeks-old data. A kept price is only asked for again once it's older than the caller allows, so it's never current: now that lookup is answered as not answered (`failed`), the Price screen shows its error with Retry, and the export falls back as it does for any card with no price today (the market price at the buy). The match is still let go, so the next ask finds the card afresh. **Retries:** an unreadable reply was retried like a server error, up to six times over about a minute, which could outlast the function's time and spend the JustTCG allowance; now it's tried once more after a second, then the lookup fails with "JustTCG's reply couldn't be read" (`_shared/justtcg.ts` `call`). Takes effect when `prices` and `secrets` are deployed. |
| 369 | A TCGplayer-only version when its search failed: a manual price, and an edit keeps its version (2026-10-19) | The v0.9.9.15-review release review, note 3, on decision 359. With the TCGplayer versions search failed (a JustTCG daily limit), a Pokémon version TCGplayer sells apart couldn't be added at all, not even at a manual price, and reloading didn't help until the limit reset at 00:00 UTC. Now a manual price (Alt+M) adds it: the price is staff's, not the plain card's, which is what 359 guarded against; without one ADD CARD says "enter a manual price (Alt+M), or reload the page to try again". Editing a line saved on such a version waited with "Its TCGplayer version didn't load" whenever the search didn't bring that version back, a failed search or the week-old one the server keeps without it; now the version is kept as saved ("As saved" in the version list), rebuilt from the line and priced by its own product, as the export already does (`lineVersion`, `todaysPrices.js`; `usePrinting.js` `savedVersion`, `PricingScreen.jsx`). |
| 368 | A price error's partial results keep only their answers (2026-10-19) | The v0.9.9.15-review release review, note 5, on decision 357. On a 429 (a JustTCG limit) or a 502, the prices function still sends what it got to, failed searches included, and the Price screen kept all of it for the page's life: a card whose search failed there showed "no price" until a reload, decision 357's bug by another way. The scan receiver kept every result the same way, on good replies too, and it shares the Price screen's cache, so since decision 360 a Magic lookup answered `failed` was kept as "no price" there as well. Now one rule keeps a reply's answers and only those (a price, or a real "no match"), on good replies and errors alike (`keepAnswers`, `src/lib/priceAnswers.js`; `usePrices.js`, `useScanReceiver.js`); the rest are asked again next time. |
| 367 | CONFIRM BUY's read-back is this tab's confirm, and a later press finds out (2026-10-19) | The v0.9.9.15-review release review, notes 1 and 2, on decision 363. **Another tab's confirm:** two tabs on one computer share its draft (spec 8.12), and the read-back took any confirm by the same user as this tab's, so a second tab pressing CONFIRM BUY after the first showed BUY COMPLETE and printed its own, possibly stale, cards, name and price. Now the buy read back must also have this tab's name, phone, notes, price paid, payment method and cards with their quantities (`readBack`, `src/pages/price/confirmReadBack.js`). Every draft write moves the buy's version and a confirm must match it, so a confirm that went through had exactly the cards this tab had; anything else is "This buy changed on another computer", as before 363. **A later press:** when the read-back itself couldn't be done (still no connection), pressing again did nothing once the draft had left the screen. Now that press is remembered: it says "Its answer was lost: it may have gone through. Press Confirm buy again to find out.", and the next press reads that buy back (on the same buy, its read-back takes either press's details, since the dialog may have changed between them). Confirmed by this tab: BUY COMPLETE with the first press's cards, name and price, and the 🚩 on that buy. Still open: "It wasn't confirmed", and the buy reloads. Finished another way: says so, and nothing is confirmed. With no buy and nothing remembered, it says there's no buy to confirm instead of nothing (`useDraft.js` `confirm`, `PricePage.jsx` `confirmBuy`). |
| 366 | Unit tests that can fail (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (B20). `roundDownPrice` and `payout` get direct tests with hand-worked values (before, `payout` only ever computed the expected value, so a rounding bug moved both sides of the check). `supabase.js` no longer needs `import.meta.env` to load, so modules that import it (`prices.js`, `cashBuys.js`, `ccExport.js`, `importCollection.js`) load under `node --test`; the Cash buys file gets its first test. The Mass Create file's test covers quotes, a line break and "+2 Mace" (kept as written, since Crystal Commerce matches the name). The stay-awake test that couldn't fail now checks the lock is let go while hidden and taken again when shown within the ten minutes. |
| 365 | Long lists are read page by page (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (B16), after decision 346: the hosted API returns at most 1,000 rows a request, and more reads than the export's could pass that. Now these read every page in a stable order (`allPages`, `inChunks.js`): the Cash buys file (unordered, a month past about 1,000 cash buys dropped buys at random; `cashBuys.js`), the Collections tab (every collection ever made), Home's "Days to export" (a backlog past 1,000 cards), the Calendar month (`buys_in_range`), and Print from a collection's ⋯ on the Collections tab (over 1,000 cards). A collection's and a day's own pages load their cards inside the buy, which the limit doesn't cut. |
| 364 | A failed first load or lock check is asked again, not read as an answer (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (B14, B17). **Collections:** a collection's first "who is editing" check that failed left it on "Checking who is editing this collection" for good, with everything blocked; now the check is asked again every 20 seconds (the lock's own beat), with one "Couldn't check who is editing this collection: trying again." A take-over that failed left the lock stuck between states, so neither the 20-second look nor another take-over ran; now it goes back to how it was and says "Couldn't take over: check the connection and try again." (`useCollectionLock.js`). **Live tables:** a failed first load of a live table counted as loaded with no data, and was never asked again, so Home said "✓ Everything's exported", the staff list was empty, a false "Master Crystal Inventory required" banner showed, the Calendar said "No cash buys" and the Collections tab was empty. Now a first load that failed stays "Loading…", and any failed load is asked again after 2, 5, 10, 20, then every 30 seconds until one works; a later failure keeps what it had (`useLiveTable.js`). |
| 363 | A CONFIRM BUY that saved but lost its answer (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (B18): on flaky wifi a CONFIRM BUY could save while its answer never came back. The screen showed "Couldn't confirm the buy", printed no BUY COMPLETE sheets and didn't set the 🚩, and pressing it again was refused because the buy was no longer a draft. Now, after an error with no answer or that refusal, the app reads the buy back; if this user confirmed it, the confirm goes on as if the answer had come: BUY COMPLETE, the printouts and the 🚩 (`useDraft.js` `confirmedBuy`). The review's other flaky-wifi findings (ADD CARD, × and creating a collection repeated after an error; a short drop with the wifi up never reloading; no request timeout) are left for later. |
| 362 | An export retried after an error (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (B13). Three ways a retry could never work, or said the wrong thing. **A buy changed elsewhere:** the dialog keeps the cards and versions it opened with (owner, 2026-10-14), so after "Something changed on another computer" its retry sent the same old versions and was refused every time. Now, when the cards themselves are unchanged (a note or a payment changed the buy), the dialog takes the buys' versions as the page has them now, keeps every pick, and says "Press Export again to go ahead"; when the cards changed, it says to Cancel and EXPORT again. Export single buy now hands its dialog the buy as the page has it, not as it was when the menu was clicked. **A lost answer (flaky wifi):** an export that saved but whose answer never came back downloaded no file, and its retry was refused with "nothing was exported", which was false. Now every export stamps an export id in each card's Sell Price basis (`cc_sell_basis.exportId`); after an error with no answer, or a retry refused as changed or already exported, the dialog reads its matched cards back (`exportLanded`, `ccExport.js`), and if they carry its id, the export went through: the file downloads from their stamps and the page reports it as exported. When it can't tell (still offline, a Can't upload cards export, or nothing matched), it says the export may have gone through and to look at the page before trying again. **A collection's file again:** a collection stays Paid/Ours after its export (decision 294), and its EXPORT said "Every Magic card here is exported already"; now it downloads the same file again from the stamps, as a Completed one does, with no user or edit lock needed. |
| 361 | A day's "same file again" is the day's export only (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (B12): EXPORT on an exported day rebuilt the file from every exported card that day, so Export single buy's cards ("101626-42") and cards a later Can't upload cards export restamped came along, and re-uploading the file added them twice. The owner: the day's export only. Now it takes the cards stamped with the day's Custom SKU (`dayExportLines`, `day/dayRules.js`); a day exported before migration 0071 (stamped the day the export ran) takes its cards with no buy number. A day whose cards were only exported one buy at a time says there's no day file to download again (a single buy's file has no re-download yet). |
| 360 | A JustTCG reply that didn't answer isn't remembered as "no match" (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (B6): the prices function read a batch reply it couldn't parse, or one with no card list, as "none of these cards exist", and a remembered card missing from a reply the same way, so every computer saw "no price" for that card for 7 days. Now an unreadable reply is tried again like a server error (`_shared/justtcg.ts` `call`); a reply with no card list answers its lookups as not answered; a remembered card missing from a reply answers with its last kept price (or not answered) and its match is let go, so the next ask finds the card by its own ids again; and a Japanese card's Poké Ball or Master Ball version (decision 225), read off its card's search, isn't "no match" when that search failed. Not-answered results carry `failed`, which the Price screen doesn't keep (decision 357). Takes effect when the `prices` function is deployed. |
| 359 | A TCGplayer version that didn't load isn't priced as the plain card (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (B9): editing a line saved on a TCGplayer-only Pokémon version, when the card's TCGplayer search failed (a daily limit, an error), quietly switched to the default version, and SAVE EDIT saved it with the default's price; and a stamped version added in the second or two before its TCGplayer search came back (or after it failed) was priced as the plain card and saved without its product, so the export priced it as plain too. Now SAVE EDIT waits with "Its TCGplayer version didn't load: reload the page to try again, or cancel the edit", and ADD CARD waits for the search ("Waiting for its TCGplayer versions") or says it failed (`PricingScreen.jsx`, `usePokemonSiblings`'s `failed`). |
| 358 | Enter doesn't add the last search's card (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (B10): the selected card stays while a new search runs (spec 8.12), so Enter pressed in the moment after typing a new search added the previous card again (merging another copy). Now ADD CARD and Enter wait until the typed search has its results: "Waiting for the search" (`PricingScreen.jsx` `searchPending`). |
| 357 | A JustTCG search that failed isn't kept (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (B7): when a name-and-number search (Pokémon without a TCGplayer product, mostly Japanese) failed, the prices function answered "no price" without remembering it, but the Price screen kept that answer for the page's life, showing no JustTCG price and letting ADD CARD save the fallback. Now such an answer isn't kept: the screen says "Couldn't load prices. Retry", and the card is asked for again when it's selected again (`didNotAnswer` in `src/lib/priceAnswers.js`, `usePrices.js`; the function marks it `failed` once deployed, decision 360). |
| 356 | The phone scanner's loose ends (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (B5): the camera could stay on after the scanning screen closed (a refocus or restart still opening when it went; `camera.js` now shuts it as it opens); Scan card pressed during an auto look acted on the screen as it was when pressed, so a Pick a printing or Which card? sheet the look opened meanwhile was ignored (`scanFrame` now reads what's open when its read starts); a card needing a price could fall out of the phone's view (the latest ten) when the computer was more than ten behind, and was never priced (`useScans.js` now always lists the scans waiting on the phone and Undos too); and an older card with hundreds of printings loaded every picture at once and kept them all (`printingMatch.js` now loads twelve at a time and keeps the last 300). |
| 355 | An older List card goes in as its List printing (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (B4): an older card whose "123/350" fitted one printing was taken at once, before the List's mark was looked at, so a List or Mystery Booster card with the mark went in as its original printing, sure, with the wrong price and export category (against the owner's "100% accuracy" for List cards, decision 329's era). Now, with the mark seen, the List's printing of that card (`plst`, "DIS-99") is taken when there is one (`printingsFor` in `src/scan/older.js`, `listPrintingOf` in `listMark.js`). |
| 354 | A stale Settings save is refused (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (B15): a Settings box saved its whole value without looking at what was saved since, so two computers (or a fast tab-and-type) overwrote each other, and a screen whose first load failed saved the built-in defaults over the real values; a box only clicked into wrote its old value back on blur. Asked which wins, the owner: **refuse the stale one**. Every Settings save now says which version it changed (`settings_save`, migration 0084); one from an out-of-date screen, or from a screen that never loaded the setting, is refused: "Settings changed on another computer: check and save again", and the screen reloads. A box left as it was when clicked into saves nothing (`useSettingField.js`). The backup time (`last_backup_at`) is written as before. |
| 353 | A walk-in's Completed side is locked, all of it (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (B11): a Completed (exported or completed-by-hand) side of a walk-in buy still let × strike or remove its bulk and placeholders, and Restore put a struck card back live into it. Now strike, × and Restore refuse every line of a side that has a Completed card (`buy_completed`; `buy_strike_line`, `buy_remove_line`, `buy_restore_line`, and Edit in project view's `buy_save_edits`, migration 0084), and the day page greys × and Restore there with the lock's reason (`BuyPanel.jsx`). Its Mark Paid/Ours again unlocks it as before. Not done here: Convert to project / collection still moves bulk and placeholders off a Completed side. |
| 352 | TCGplayer-only cards from two sets stay apart (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (B8): the merge rule (`internal.same_card`) compared Scryfall and TCGdex ids, so two Pokémon cards known only from TCGplayer (neither id), with the same name, finish, condition and price, from two sets, became one line of the first. Now two cards with neither id are one card only with the same set, number, TCGplayer product and JustTCG card (migration 0084). |
| 351 | A scan merged into a line is found again (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (B3, part): a phone's scan whose copy merged into an existing line left no trace of itself there, so when its "added" answer was lost and it was claimed again, it was added a second time. A merge now records the scan's id on the line (`price_snapshot.scans`; `draft_add_line`, `collection_add_line`, migration 0084), and `lineOfScan` finds it (`src/lib/scanPrice.js`). The review's other B3 cases (an Undo after a lost answer, FOIL? leaving none, notes after Undo) are app fixes for later. |
| 350 | A walk-in's scans go into their own buy, or the phone says so (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (B1, B2): a walk-in scan left waiting (an Unpair, a card still being priced when CONFIRM BUY finished) went into whatever buy was next on that computer, the next customer's, under the earlier user. Now a walk-in scan remembers the buy it was scanned for (`scans.target_draft_id`; with none open yet, the scan opens it, so every walk-in scan has one). Asked what happens to it once that buy is confirmed or cancelled, the owner: **the phone shows it, to rescan**. Confirm and Cancel mark its scans not added by then as failed, "Buy N was confirmed first: scan it again for the next buy" (or "The buy was cancelled…"), which the phone lists; the computer adds a scan's card only to the buy it was for (`draft_add_line`'s `p_draft_id`, which locks the buy and is refused `draft_gone` once it's confirmed or gone, a CONFIRM BUY in flight included), and claims only its own buy's scans (`scan_claim`'s `p_draft_id`; an Undo whatever the buy) (migration 0084, `useScanReceiver.js`, `useDraft.js`, `PricePage.jsx`). |
| 349 | Buy numbers in order, without gaps (2026-10-19) | The owner, seeing Buy 137 then Buy 154 and the next buy due as Buy 224: "We have to make it so database test runs don't use up real numbers and renumber all the current buys to be in order without skips." Numbers came from a sequence (0071), which isn't rolled back, so every database test run used up its test buys' numbers (decision 342 had stopped new ones). Asked how to number from now on, the owner: **the highest so far + 1**, taken in the same save as CONFIRM BUY (and Convert to buy…), one at a time under a lock, so a test run or a failed confirm leaves no gap. A deleted or converted buy's number isn't given again, except the newest's when nothing has raised the floor to it since (a restore, a test run's restore test). A database test run holds that lock from its first test buy, so CONFIRM BUY waits for it: run it when the store isn't buying. The old sequence is now only a floor no confirm moves: a restore raises it past the restored buys (decision 348), and SETUP.md raises it by hand past numbers printed after a backup. Asked about the buys there were, the owner: **renumber them 1, 2, 3… oldest first, with the Changelog to match**: the 43 buys (1–154) became 1–43 (only 137 → 42 and 154 → 43 changed; the list is in `C:\ClaudeProjects\temp\buy-renumber-2026-10-19.txt`), and the Changelog titles of each buy that still exists name its new number (84 entries, 70 of them still carrying the per-day numbers from before 0071); entries of buys that are gone keep the numbers they were written with (55 titles, 4 summaries), so a search for a number can also show a gone buy's history. Printouts already made show the old numbers. The open item "a project converted back to a buy keeps its number" will need the number kept on the project row, so it isn't given out meanwhile (closed by decision 385: it takes the next number). No Export single buy SKU used a buy number. Migration 0083 (`internal.next_buy_number`). |
| 348 | A backup restores into a new project (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (finding A7): disaster recovery didn't work end to end. Now: a restore moves the buy-number counter past the highest restored number (in a new project it started at 1 and every CONFIRM BUY collided); an older backup restores, each column it lacks taking its default instead of NULL (a backup from before a NOT NULL column failed); backups hold the matches staff taught Crystal Commerce (`cc_product_links`, `cc_set_map`, `import_links`), restored when the file has them (the owner: in backups; prod still starts without them, decision 332), migration 0082. Restore takes two presses: the first downloads the safety backup of what's there, which a browser can't confirm saved, and says to check it; the second restores, if it comes within two minutes (later, a fresh safety backup first: what other computers saved meanwhile would be in neither file) and isn't a double-click (`BackupsPanel.jsx`). `docs/SETUP.md` has the steps for restoring into a new project, including moving the counter past buy numbers used after the backup by hand. Not done: a backup with columns the database lacks still restores without them (silently). |
| 347 | The "another card" refusal on every answer (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (finding A6): the prices function refuses a TCGplayer product that is another card (decision 288), but only when it asked JustTCG by the product itself. Every card TCGdex gives that product shares one lookup key, so once the product's own card was priced (or a TCGplayer search found it), the wrong card's lookup came back from the remembered match or the shared cache unchecked and was priced as the other card, for good. Now the check runs on every answer for a Pokémon product lookup: cached, remembered or fresh (`refusedFor` in `supabase/functions/prices/index.ts`), and the browser checks its own page-long cache the same way, by number, before using it (`src/lib/anotherCard.js`, `usePrices.js`). The cached answer stays right for the product's own card. Takes effect on the server once the `prices` function is deployed. |
| 346 | Product candidates read page by page (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (finding A5): the export read every line's Crystal Commerce candidates in one call (`cc_candidates`), and the hosted API returns at most its "Max rows" (1,000 by default) per request. Past about 300 Magic lines (a big project, an 837-row import) the lines after the cut got no candidates and went to Can't upload cards, and a line cut part-way could auto-pick the one candidate left. The export, its audit print and Import Collection now read the candidates page by page, in a fixed order, until a page comes back empty, so any server limit reads everything (`allPages` in `src/lib/inChunks.js`). SQUASH.md section 10 has a step to set prod's Max rows to dev's. The other unpaged reads (launch review B16) are for later. |
| 345 | An exported card is frozen (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (finding A1): the guarantee that an exported card never changes rested on EXPORT making the collection Completed. Since decision 294 a project exports in parts and stays Paid/Ours, and an unlocked collection keeps its stamps, but nothing else looked at the stamp: a new copy (the phone's too) merged into an exported line and was never sent; an edit folded lines together, sending cards twice or never; strike and restore moved copies between exported and unexported lines; × left a Can't upload copy at the wrong count. Asked, the owner chose: **a card with an export stamp (exported, or in Can't upload cards) is frozen**, and × on it refuses rather than also taking its copy out of Can't upload; Undo export stays whole (decision 298). Only export and its undo change such a card now. Clicking it to edit, ×, Restore and SAVE EDIT say "That card is exported: Undo export… first to change it" (`line_exported`); REPRICE leaves it; new copies and edits never merge into it, nor strikes and restores into or out of it (`collection_add_line`, `collection_update_line`, `collection_strike_line`, `collection_remove_line`, `collection_restore_line`, `collection_reprice`, `internal.same_line`, migration 0081; `BuyList.jsx`, `RepriceModal.jsx`). A file-only EXPORT leaves out the cards already exported. Set Rate still works on it (rates don't reach the export). |
| 344 | One card, one copy, on slow wifi (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (finding A3): the phone could send the same card twice. A look gives up at 10 s (decision 300) while its send can retry for 15 s (decision 302), and the next look sent the same card again with a new id; a send that lost its answer to a dropped connection wasn't retried at all, and auto scan sent it again. Now the phone sends a card only once at a time: while its send is out, the same printing and finish isn't sent again (a second press says "Still sending that card: wait for its chime"), and a send with no sure answer keeps its id, so trying again, by retry, by the next look or by pressing Scan card, finds the scan already saved (`scan_send` keeps one per id, decision 302). A lost connection is retried like a time-out, with the same id (`src/scan/Scanner.jsx` `sendCard`, `src/scan/useScans.js` `send`). Help's "a card sent twice over a bad connection goes in once" now holds. |
| 343 | Leaving the price screen keeps the phone's waiting scans (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (finding A2): `scan_session_end` deleted the computer's session, and its scans went with it, so every card the phone had chimed for and the computer hadn't added yet was lost when the screen went: Help, the Calendar, Settings, F5, another collection. Deleting the collection a session named did the same to every waiting scan on that computer. Now a scan belongs to the computer, not its session (its key points at `devices`), so ending or deleting a session leaves it, and it remembers the list it was sent to (`scans.target_kind`, `target_buy_id`). Only that list claims it: the one `scan_claim` names (`p_kind`, `p_buy_id`), or with none named the one the computer's session has open now. A card scanned for one collection never lands in another; the waiting cards come in when the phone pairs with that list again (pairing needs a screen that can add, so never into a locked, view-only or still-loading list). A batch stops when its screen goes, leaving the rest waiting, and the phone shows only the scans for the list it's paired with (migration 0080, `useScanReceiver.js`, `src/scan/useScans.js`). Unclaimed scans are swept after a week, as before. Walk-in scans have no buy id: a walk-in's waiting cards can still go into the next customer's buy on that computer (finding B2, for later). |
| 342 | The test run uses up no buy numbers; prod starts at Buy 1 (2026-10-19) | The full launch review (finding A4): a database test run confirmed 18 test buys, and a sequence isn't rolled back, so every run used up 18 real buy numbers on dev (the live store, after every migration) and would have made prod's first buy about Buy 19. `scripts/db-test-run.mjs` now puts `buys_number_seq` back after the run, or just past the highest real buy if one was confirmed meanwhile, and prints "Buy numbers put back: the next buy is Buy N". Asked where prod's buy numbers should start, the owner: **at 1**. Dev-era numbers may then repeat on labels; Custom SKUs carry the date, so Crystal Commerce doesn't collide. |
| 341 | A converted buy's payment is counted once (2026-10-19) | The launch-readiness review (finding A3): **Convert to project** and **Convert to collection** copied the buy's price paid and method onto the new project or collection. When Completed cards stay behind, the buy stays too with its own payment, so the month's **Cash buys** file listed the cash twice ($100 paid showed as $200). Asked which row carries the payment, the owner: the original buy only. While the buy stays, the new project or collection is paid **$0** with the buy's method (not left empty: a Paid/Ours collection with no payment reads "TBD", and its colour and Paid/Ours Cash / Credit filters go by the method). When nothing is left on the buy, it's deleted and the new one carries the payment as before (`buy_to_project`, `buy_to_collection`, migration 0079). The convert dialog says the payment stays with the buy. No conversion on dev kept its buy. This amends decision 335's "what was paid and when", decision 228's "a project made from a buy kept its … price paid" and section 10.2's Convert to project: all three hold only when the whole buy moves. Unlocking such a collection and marking it Paid/Ours again asks for a price as usual; typing the buy's payment there would count it twice. |

A name alone keeps its usual order. A number alone, with no name or set, is as before (every List card would come). `scryfall.js` (`printsQuery`, `keepListed`, `listedNumber`), `cardSearch.js` ranking. **Note (2026-10-21, smoke pass):** a set code typed before the number ("rvr 26") finds nothing, and that's as meant. | 328 | ~~Stamped promos found by their stamp~~ (2026-10-18; removed by decision 400) | The owner, with Aziza, Mage Tower Captain (promo pack, the planeswalker stamp) and Nashi, Searcher in the Dark (prerelease, "20★24"): "Can we try to detect this information quickly so that we can suggest/add it to cards that might have stamps but otherwise have the same collector info? In Magic stamped cards are an entirely different printing categorically, so this matters as much as set detection in a way." Then: "Build all of that, but skip the STAMP? button that's not needed, that can be done on the PC if needed." Their collector line is the plain card's. On Scryfall they're in the set's promo set: the number with "p" (promo pack) or "s" (prerelease, foil only). Now, for a card the strip named (2015 frame, not a promo, not The List) whose set's promo set has a stamped printing of that number and name (one search per set a page's life, `e:p<set> (is:promopack or is:prerelease)`), the phone compares two spots of the card in the camera, the art's lower right (planeswalker stamp) and lower left (the year), with the plain card's Scryfall picture, each spot tried 2% each way in 0.5% steps and its best match kept. One spot past 0.53 and that printing exists: the stamped printing goes in instead (a prerelease as foil). Both past it, or neither: the plain one. Scryfall's pictures of the stamped printings aren't compared: Aziza 174p's has the stamp about 18 px higher (of 680) than the printed card. Measured on the two cards, outline off by up to 3%: stamped spots 0.59 or more, unstamped 0.47 or less. Four seconds at most, else the plain printing. No STAMP? button; the computer changes the printing if needed. Every check goes in the run log (`stamp`: the two spots, the printings, what was picked, how long), to set the cutoff from a real run. `stamps.js`, `Scanner.jsx`. |

| 322 | Read and re-crop once the card has settled (2026-10-18) | The owner: "it seems across the board the first read is happening slightly too early when waiting for blur to clear after motion … there's 1 wasted early read often that is then, redone", and the collector box still worse past 100 cards. On the screenshot's still picture a fresh crop cuts both collector lines whole, so the bad boxes came from moments of motion. Now auto scan reads only when the card's art is the same as a look ago (it settled), and the re-crop waits for a settled look too; the first read comes one look later, on a sharp picture, instead of a misread and a redo. | 321 | Stop is an abort (2026-10-18) | The owner: "The stop button on the phone should cut off the camera faster than it does, as an abort button, do what needs to be done to stop the camera immediately." Stop now acts on the touch, not the lift (a click waits for the finger, and for a look's picture work under way); shuts the camera's tracks (disabled first) and empties the video; cancels any opening under way (START, a refocus, a restart after freezing, the tab shown again), whose camera, if it opens after Stop, is shut at once and never shown (before, a refocus or restart finishing after Stop brought the camera back); and gives up the look and any Scan card read under way and stops the card reader, so nothing goes on working behind it. The view's lock stays for when the camera's back. `camera.js` `generation`, `Scanner.jsx` `abortCamera`. |
| 320 | The computer stays awake while cards arrive (2026-10-18) | The owner: "A card arriving on the PC screen should ping the computer to stay awake like a keystroke would, right now it sleeps on me when I'm scanning." A page can't press a key, but it can hold a screen wake lock (`navigator.wakeLock`, Chrome, Edge, Safari 16.4+), which keeps the display on and a Mac awake with it. Each card arriving from the phone keeps it for 10 minutes more, as a keystroke would; 10 quiet minutes and it's let go, and the computer sleeps as it would have. The browser drops the lock when the tab is hidden; it's taken again when the tab's shown while still wanted. A browser without it (or refusing it, a battery saver) sleeps as before. `lib/stayAwake.js`, `useScanReceiver.js`. |

| 318 | 🔔 off and on again starts the sound afresh (2026-10-18) | The owner: "My phone stopped making the sound again, can we make toggling the sound icon back and forth a hard reset for sound that always makes it refresh no matter why the iphone has it muted?" Now turning the sounds back on (🔇 → 🔔) closes the audio and starts a new one in that tap, asks Safari to play the page's sound as media (`navigator.audioSession.type = 'playback'`, so the ringer switch doesn't silence it), plays a moment of silence through an `<audio>` element (the same on an iPhone without audioSession), then plays the chime, so it's heard to be back. The run log notes each (`sound-reset`). `scan/sounds.js` `resetSounds`. |

| 309 | A second read for white titles (2026-10-18) | The owner, on cards like Mercadian Masques that stayed unread: "Let's test this [a contrast-boosted second read of the title]". The old frames' titles are white letters with a dark shadow; the usual cleanup keeps the smaller share of the band, so on a light parchment bar it kept the shadow (hollow letters) and on Mercadian Masques' blue marble it kept the marbling. The second read takes each pixel's darkest colour channel (white is bright in all three; parchment, marble and red frames aren't) and keeps what's past Otsu's threshold and past 80% of the band's brightest. Measured on the 32 older cards from Scryfall (decision 308) with the title found: 26 named before, 31 with a fixed brightness cut (200 of 255) but 0 rescued once the cards were dimmed to 75% or 60% as a phone photo is, so it's the relative cut: 30, 30 and 30 of 32 on the clean, 75% and 60% sets (rescuing Statecraft and Misdirection, Mercadian Masques, Coalition Honor Guard, Keldon Champion, Serra Aviary). Order: the usual read and the white read at the likeliest place, the same at the next, the usual at the third, a block read last: six at most. | 308 | An older card's title found, not assumed (2026-10-18) | The owner, with a screenshot of Courser of Kruphix (BNG, 2014) whose info box showed only the frame's top line: "Sometimes for older cards the name reading is misaligned in a way that makes it so it can't read the name at all. The title was read from a fixed band, 3.5–10.5% down the card's outline; an outline a little off the card, or a frame whose title bar sits lower (this one's is 6.5–12.5%), put the band on the border. Now the band is looked for: down the top of the card, from 5% above the outline's top to a fifth of the way down, the place where a line of text sits, busy across its middle and calm above and below (the art and the border being busy or calm all the way through); the three likeliest places are read in turn as one line till one names a card, then the best as a block. The info box shows the band found. Its left edge moved from 6.5% to 4.5% in (the C of "Courser" was cut). Measured on 32 random older cards from Scryfall (8 each of the 1993, 1997, 2003 and 2008–2014 frames), on a wider background: the found band named 25, 25 and 26 of them with the outline exact, 6% too high and 4% too low; the fixed band 21, 0 and 0. | 307 | The computer's side never stuck either (2026-10-18) | The owner: "Fix, add timers to the PC side as well so we don't get stuck." Now: looking for scans, claiming one and telling the phone how it went 5 s each (a database round trip measured 0.17 s at worst), adding or taking off the line 10 s, the exchange rate 5 s (measured 0.15 s; without it the Cardmarket step has no rate), the JustTCG price function 15 s (not measurable from here: it needs the store's sign-in; without it, on to Cardmarket as on any JustTCG failure), a whole scan 30 s ("It took too long on <computer>: check its list, then scan it again"); a scan given up adds nothing late. A look or a claim with no answer is tried on the next message or poll. On the phone, a scan still "sending…" or "pricing…" 15 s after the phone first saw it says "still waiting on <computer>: is its price screen open?" or "<computer> is still pricing it…", in amber. `useScanReceiver.js`, `Scanner.jsx` `STILL_WAITING_MS`. |
| 306 | Too dark: a hint (2026-10-18) | The owner, to "No hint in dim light": "fix". A dark picture read badly and the scanner only said it couldn't read the card. Each look measures the light in the locked card's view (the whole picture before the lock) from a 48 × 48 copy: not the average (the camera brightens a dim picture, and a black-bordered card is dark in any light) but how bright its brightest 5% gets; under 90 of 255 is too dark. Four dark looks in a row and the strip says "Too dark to read well: tap 💡 for the light" (or "more light on the card helps" with no torch, or the torch on), once, gone when the light's back; a Scan card that fails on a dark picture says "Couldn't read this card: it's too dark. Tap 💡 for the light, then press Scan card again." Reading carries on either way. `scan/light.js`. |
| 305 | The card reader restarted now and then (2026-10-18) | The owner, to "The card reader is never restarted": "fix". One Tesseract worker ran for the whole session, and it uses more memory the longer it runs. After every 300 reads it's swapped for a fresh one between two reads; the new one starts loading straight away (from the browser's cache), so the next read waits a moment at most. Stop and hold-to-start-over still start it afresh at once (decision 300). `scan/ocr.js` `RECYCLE_AFTER`. |
| 304 | Auto scan rests when nothing's new (2026-10-18) | The owner, to "Auto scan never slows down": "fix". Auto scan looked every 0.3 s while the camera was on, an empty holder included (the spec expected a warm phone after a few hundred cards). After 30 s with nothing new (no card found, the card's art unchanged from the last look, no button pressed, the camera not just started) it looks once a second; anything new and it's back to every 0.3 s straight away. A card laid in after a rest is read within about a second. `Scanner.jsx` `IDLE_AFTER_MS`, `IDLE_REST_MS`. |
| 303 | The camera cut off (2026-10-18) | The owner, to "The camera doesn't notice when it's cut off": "fix". A call, Siri or another app could take the camera while the page stayed open; the picture froze and auto scan kept reading it. Now the camera's track is watched: **paused by the phone** (the track muted), auto scan waits and the strip says "The phone has taken the camera (a call?): scanning carries on when it gives it back"; **ended by the phone**, or **frozen** (the video's clock hasn't moved for 3 s, checked every second, as an iPhone doesn't always say), the camera starts again by itself, saying "The camera stopped: starting it again…"; more than 3 restarts in a minute and it's left off: "The camera keeps stopping. Press START CAMERA to try again." The view's lock is kept through it. `scan/camera.js`. |
| 302 | A scan saved once (2026-10-18) | The owner, to "No protection against sending the same card twice": "fix". Since decision 300 the phone gives up on a send after 5 s; the send may have landed anyway, and pressing Scan card again added a second copy. Each scan now carries the phone's own id for it (`scans.client_id`, unique per phone); a send with no answer is tried again up to twice with the same id (15 s in all), and scan_send returns the scan already saved under it instead of saving another. Only after the third try does the phone say "No answer from the computer after three tries: check its list before scanning this card again." Migration 0075. |

| 300 | The scanner: never stuck on Reading…, Stop and start over (2026-10-18) | The owner, after a day at work: "it kept getting stuck on 'Reading...' after hitting Scan Card. It was on a slower network … stuck on Reading... forever until a hard refresh"; "we need a button or some gesture that helps it get unstuck". **Why it stuck:** no request had a time limit. Scryfall's requests go one at a time through one queue, so a request that stalled on a bad connection held every lookup after it; a look held the scanner's busy flag till it ended, so auto scan never looked again and Scan card waited behind it showing Reading…; the card reader (Tesseract) works the same way, one read at a time. **Fixed:** every request through the app's transport is given up when it has no answer in time and tried again like a network error; a Tesseract read is given up and the reader started afresh; the phone's writes (send, answer, undo) give up, sending saying "No answer from the computer yet: check its list before scanning this card again"; a whole look gives up and says so. The limits are about ten times the slowest measured on 2026-10-18 (the owner: "measure the examples on this network … set the timeout durations more tightly"), rounded up to the next 5 s ("to be safe"): a Scryfall lookup 5 s (slowest first byte 0.32 s), a Scryfall search 10 s (0.97 s), TCGdex 10 s (0.67 s), the Pokémon TCG API 5 s (0.28 s), a body 10 s (0.85 s after its first byte), a phone write 5 s (database round trip 0.17 s, plus the write), a Tesseract read 5 s (0.3–1 s on a phone), loading the reader 20 s (4.4 MB in 1.35 s, plus compiling it on the phone), a whole Scan card or Title 20 s (its slowest honest run about 15 s), an auto look 10 s (about 4 s); a look given up does nothing more if it ever ends (it can't send a card late). A quick Scan card (taking a very sure strip read before the full run) was built and then dropped the same day (the owner: "with the rest of these safeguards I don't think I want a quick scan feature at all"): Scan card always does the full run. **Unstuck:** Reading… turns into **Reading… tap to stop** after 3 s: tapping gives the read up, starts the reader afresh and forgets what auto scan held about the last card, so the card in the holder is read again. **Holding Scan card** (0.7 s, a buzz) does the same and reads the card now; the view stays locked (Reset view unlocks it). Scan card with the camera not ready says so instead of nothing. `lib/timeout.js`, `lib/transport.js`, `scan/ocr.js` `resetOcr`, `scan/Scanner.jsx`. |
| 299 | A search in Help (2026-10-18) | The owner: "a small search bar on the header of the page panel, right aligned, that is a simple word search for the document and when searched shows pages that have that word on them in order of relevance." A **Search Help…** box at the right of the page's header (after "N of M"). Typing lists, under it, the pages that have every word typed (two letters or more; a word matches where a word starts, so "port" finds "portrait", not "export"; accents ignored, so "pokemon" finds "Pokémon"), most relevant first: a word in the page's title counts 10, in one of its headings 4, in its text 1 for each time; ties keep the Help's order. Each result shows its title, chapter, how many times the words are on it and a line around the first one. ↓ ↑ and Enter or a click open the page and scroll to the first word; Esc clears the box. While the words are in the box they're highlighted on every page (the browser's own highlights; the page's HTML is untouched). `lib/help.js` `searchHelp`, `src/pages/help/HelpSearch.jsx`. |

Undoing a collection's export has no button now: flagged. Decision 182 superseded. | 288 | The name search by the set's TCGplayer home; a product that is another card is refused (2026-10-17) | From the audit (`PKM_PRICES.md` 3.3, 3.4): 35% of TCGdex's English versions (9,222 cards: Scarlet & Violet base, Sword & Shield base, every Sun & Moon, XY, Black & White and Diamond & Pearl set, promos) carry no TCGplayer product and go through the `prices` function's name search, where 1,083 could never match (TCGdex "Diamond & Pearl" vs TCGplayer "Diamond and Pearl", "Sun & Moon" vs "SM Base Set", every promo set) and Base Set was always ambiguous.
